   905→+fn is_attachable_session(entry: &SessionEntry) -> bool {
   906→+    !entry.archived && !entry.key.starts_with("cron:")
   907→+}
   908→+
   909→+fn session_list_label(entry: &SessionEntry) -> &str {
   910→+    entry.label.as_deref().unwrap_or(&entry.key)
   911→+}
   912→+
   913→+fn format_channel_sessions_list(sessions: &[SessionEntry], current_session_key: &str) -> String {
   914→+    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   915→+    for (i, session) in sessions.iter().enumerate() {
   916→+        let marker = if session.key == current_session_key {
   917→+            " *"
   918→+        } else {
   919→+            ""
   920→+        };
   921→+        lines.push(format!(
   922→+            "{}. {} ({} msgs){}",
   923→+            i + 1,
   924→+            session_list_label(session),
   925→+            session.message_count,
   926→+            marker,
   927→+        ));
   928→+    }
   929→+    lines.push("\nUse /sessions N to switch.".to_string());
   930→+    lines.join("\n")
   931→+}
   932→+
   933→+fn format_attachable_sessions_list(sessions: &[SessionEntry], current_session_key: &str) -> String {
   934→+    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   935→+    for (i, session) in sessions.iter().enumerate() {
   936→+        let label = session_list_label(session);
   937→+        let marker = if session.key == current_session_key {
   938→+            " *"
   939→+        } else {
   940→+            ""
   941→+        };
   942→+        let key_suffix = if label == session.key {
   943→+            String::new()
   944→+        } else {
   945→+            format!(" [{}]", session.key)
   946→+        };
   947→+        lines.push(format!(
   948→+            "{}. {}{} ({} msgs){}",
   949→+            i + 1,
   950→+            label,
   951→+            key_suffix,
   952→+            session.message_count,
   953→+            marker,
   954→+        ));
   955→+    }
   956→+    lines.push(
   957→+        "\nUse /attach N to move an existing session to this chat. This rebinds it from any previous channel chat."
   958→+            .to_string(),
   959→+    );
   960→+    lines.join("\n")
   961→+}
   962→+
   963→+fn format_pending_approvals_list(requests: &[PendingApprovalView]) -> String {
   964→+    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   965→+    for (i, request) in requests.iter().enumerate() {
   966→+        lines.push(format!("{}. `{}`", i + 1, request.command));
   967→+    }
   968→+    lines.push("\nUse /approve N or /deny N.".to_string());
   969→+    lines.join("\n")
   970→+}

  1034→+
  1035→+fn resolve_channel_session_defaults_from_config(
  1036→+    config: &serde_json::Value,
  1037→+    chat_id: &str,
  1038→+    sender_id: Option<&str>,
  1039→+) -> ChannelSessionDefaults {
  1040→+    let user_override = override_map(
  1041→+        config,
  1042→+        "user_overrides",
  1043→+        sender_id
  1044→+            .filter(|sender_id| *sender_id != chat_id)
  1045→+            .unwrap_or(chat_id),
  1046→+    );
  1047→+    let channel_override = override_map(config, "channel_overrides", chat_id);
  1048→+
  1049→+    ChannelSessionDefaults {
  1050→+        model: user_override
  1051→+            .and_then(|override_value| config_string(override_value.get("model")))
  1052→+            .or_else(|| {
  1053→+                channel_override
  1054→+                    .and_then(|override_value| config_string(override_value.get("model")))
  1055→+            })
  1056→+            .or_else(|| config_string(config.get("model"))),
  1057→+        agent_id: user_override
  1058→+            .and_then(|override_value| config_string(override_value.get("agent_id")))
  1059→+            .or_else(|| {
  1060→+                channel_override
  1061→+                    .and_then(|override_value| config_string(override_value.get("agent_id")))
  1062→+            })
  1063→+            .or_else(|| config_string(config.get("agent_id"))),
  1064→+    }
  1065→+}

  3188→ ## Access Control
  3189→ 
  3190→@@ -211,6 +215,24 @@ after `stream_min_initial_chars` characters (default: 30) and then edits it

  3193→ 
  3194→+## Session Commands
  3195→+
  3196→+Telegram supports the standard channel session commands:
  3197→+
  3198→+| Command | Description |
  3199→+|---------|-------------|
  3200→+| `/new` | Start a fresh session for the current chat |
  3201→+| `/sessions` | List or switch among sessions already bound to the current chat |
  3202→+| `/attach` | List existing non-cron sessions and rebind one to the current chat |
  3203→+| `/approvals` | List pending exec approvals for the current session |
  3204→+| `/approve N` | Approve the numbered exec request from `/approvals` |
  3205→+| `/deny N` | Deny the numbered exec request from `/approvals` |
  3206→+
  3207→+`/sessions` is intentionally scoped to the current chat. If you want to bring a
  3208→+different existing session into the chat, use `/attach` instead. Reattaching a
  3209→+session moves that session's channel binding to the current chat, it is not a
  3210→+copy.
  3211→+
  3212→ Set `stream_mode = "off"` to disable streaming and send the full response as a
  3213→ single message.

  3247→+WhatsApp also supports optional per-chat and per-user overrides for models and

  3262→ ```
  3263→ 
  3264→ ## Access Control
  3265→@@ -177,10 +200,18 @@ WhatsApp uses the same access control model as Telegram channels.
  3266→ 
  3267→ | Policy | Behavior |
  3268→ |--------|----------|
  3269→-| `open` | Bot responds in all groups it's part of |
  3270→-| `allowlist` | Bot only responds in groups on the `group_allowlist` |
  3271→+| `open` | Bot responds in all groups it's part of, subject to `mention_mode` |
  3272→+| `allowlist` | Bot only responds in groups on the `group_allowlist`, subject to `mention_mode` |
  3273→ | `disabled` | Bot ignores all group messages |
  3274→ 
  3275→+### Mention Mode
  3276→+
  3277→+| Mode | Behavior |
  3278→+|------|----------|
  3279→+| `always` | Bot may respond to allowed group messages without an @mention |
  3280→+| `mention` | Bot only responds in allowed groups when the account is @mentioned |
  3281→+| `none` | Bot never responds in groups |

  3283→ ### OTP Self-Approval

> AGENT

Here's my security review of all changes on this branch:

---

## Security Review: `dorian-pepperberry`

### 1. `/attach` command — Session hijacking risk (HIGH)

**File:** `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs:1461-1600`

The `/attach` command lets any channel user list **all** non-archived, non-cron sessions via `session_metadata.list()` and rebind any of them to their current chat. There is **no authorization check** verifying the sender should have access to the target session.

**Impact:**
- A Telegram group member could attach a web UI session or another channel's private session to their chat, gaining access to the conversation history and ongoing agent context.
- `clear_active_session_mappings` removes the session from its previous chat binding, so this is also a denial-of-service to the original user.
- The session list exposes labels and keys of all sessions to any channel user.

**Recommendation:** Filter the attachable session list to sessions the caller created or sessions that belong to the same channel/account. At minimum, restrict to sessions that already have a channel binding for the same channel type + account, or require the session to be unbound.

---

### 2. `/approve` and `/deny` — No sender authorization (MEDIUM-HIGH)

**File:** `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs:1527-1601`

Any user who can send messages to the channel chat can approve or deny exec commands for the bound session. The `sender_id` is passed to `dispatch_command` but is **never checked** against any authorization list in the approve/deny handlers.

**Impact:**
- In a group chat, any group member can approve dangerous shell commands (e.g., `rm -rf /`, `curl` exfil payloads) that the agent has requested approval for.
- The approval notification (`crates/gateway/src/approval.rs:136-148`) sends the full command text to the channel, which could leak sensitive information (paths, credentials in command args) to all group members.

**Recommendation:** Check that the sender is the session owner or is on a dedicated approval allowlist. Consider restricting approval commands to DMs or to the session creator. At minimum, suppress full command text in group notifications.

---

### 3. Approval notification leaks command text to channels (LOW-MEDIUM)

**File:** `crates/gateway/src/approval.rs:136-140`

```rust
let message = format!(
    "Approval needed for `{}`.\nUse /approvals ...",
    command
);
```

The full command string is broadcast to the originating channel. If the command contains sensitive paths, filenames, or embedded secrets (e.g., `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer sk-..."`) these are exposed to all chat participants.

**Recommendation:** Truncate or sanitize the command in notifications, or only show a summary/ID rather than the full command string in group contexts.

---

### 4. MS Teams missing `agent_id` resolution (LOW)

**File:** `crates/msteams/src/plugin.rs:474`

MS Teams hardcodes `agent_id: None` rather than using `config.resolve_agent_id(...)`. This is inconsistent with all other channels (Discord, Telegram, Slack, WhatsApp, Matrix) which now resolve agent IDs through the config view. Not a vulnerability, but means MS Teams silently ignores agent routing config.

---

### 5. Items that look correct

- **WhatsApp `should_ignore_inbound_chat`** — Good addition, prevents processing status broadcasts and broadcast lists.
- **WhatsApp mention mode** — Access checks in `check_group_access` correctly apply mention gating *after* group policy, so `MentionMode::None` overrides even allowed groups. Tests cover this.
- **Agent ID fallback** — `resolve_channel_agent_id` properly validates the requested agent exists and falls back to the default with a warning. No trust-the-input vulnerability.
- **SQL parameterization** — `clear_active_session_mappings` uses `?` bind parameter, no injection risk.
- **Session key in approvals** — Proper scoping via `pending_requests_for_session` prevents cross-session approval listing.
- **`sender_id` propagation** — All channel handlers now pass `sender_id` through to `dispatch_command`, which is good plumbing for future authorization.

---

### Summary

| Issue | Severity | Fix Effort |
|-------|----------|------------|
| `/attach` exposes and rebinds any session without auth | **High** | Medium — add ownership/scope filter |
| `/approve`/`/deny` no sender authorization | **Medium-High** | Medium — check sender against owner |
| Approval notification leaks full command text | **Low-Medium** | Low — truncate or redact |
| MS Teams missing agent_id resolution | **Low** | Low — add `resolve_agent_id` call |

The `/attach` and `/approve` issues are the most concerning since they could allow unauthorized access to sessions and unauthorized approval of shell commands in group contexts.

> DEVELOPER

Please proceed fixing all issues you found

   100→}
   101→
   102→/// Broadcasts approval requests to connected WebSocket clients.
   103→pub struct GatewayApprovalBroadcaster {
   104→    state: Arc<GatewayState>,
   105→}
   106→
   107→impl GatewayApprovalBroadcaster {
   108→    pub fn new(state: Arc<GatewayState>) -> Self {
   109→        Self { state }
   110→    }
   111→
   112→    async fn notify_origin_channel(
   113→        &self,
   114→        session_key: Option<&str>,
   115→        command: &str,
   116→    ) -> moltis_tools::Result<()> {
   117→        let Some(session_key) = session_key else {
   118→            return Ok(());
   119→        };
   120→        let Some(session_metadata) = self.state.services.session_metadata.as_ref() else {
   121→            return Ok(());
   122→        };
   123→        let Some(outbound) = self.state.services.channel_outbound_arc() else {
   124→            return Ok(());
   125→        };
   126→
   127→        let target = session_metadata
   128→            .get(session_key)
   129→            .await
   130→            .and_then(|entry| channel_reply_target_for_entry(&entry));
   131→        let Some(target) = target else {
   132→            return Ok(());
   133→        };
   134→
   135→        let message = format!(
   136→            "Approval needed for `{}`.\nUse /approvals to see the numbered list, then /approve N or /deny N. The web UI still works too.",
   137→            command
   138→        );
   139→        outbound
   140→            .send_text(&target.account_id, &target.outbound_to(), &message, None)
   141→            .await
   142→            .map_err(|error| moltis_tools::Error::external("send approval notification", error))
   143→    }
   144→}
   145→
   146→#[async_trait]
   147→impl ApprovalBroadcaster for GatewayApprovalBroadcaster {
   148→    async fn broadcast_request(
   149→        &self,
   150→        request_id: &str,
   151→        command: &str,
   152→        session_key: Option<&str>,
   153→    ) -> moltis_tools::Result<()> {
   154→        broadcast(
   155→            &self.state,
   156→            "exec.approval.requested",
   157→            serde_json::json!({
   158→                "requestId": request_id,
   159→                "command": command,
   160→                "sessionKey": session_key,
   161→            }),
   162→            BroadcastOpts::default(),
   163→        )
   164→        .await;
   165→        if let Err(error) = self.notify_origin_channel(session_key, command).await {
   166→            warn!(%error, session_key, request_id, "failed to notify originating channel about approval");
   167→        }
   168→        Ok(())
   169→    }
   170→}
   171→
   172→fn channel_reply_target_for_entry(entry: &SessionEntry) -> Option<ChannelReplyTarget> {
   173→    entry
   174→        .channel_binding
   175→        .as_deref()
   176→        .and_then(|binding| serde_json::from_str(binding).ok())
   177→}
   178→
   179→#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::expect_used)]

    93→
    94→fn rewrite_for_shell_mode(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    95→    let trimmed = text.trim();
    96→    if trimmed.is_empty() {
    97→        return None;
    98→    }
    99→
   100→    if let Some(cmd) = slash_command_name(trimmed)
   101→        && is_channel_control_command_name(cmd)
   102→    {
   103→        return None;
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    Some(format!("/sh {trimmed}"))
   107→}
   108→
   109→fn parse_numbered_selection(arg: &str, command_name: &str) -> ChannelResult<usize> {
   110→    arg.parse()
   111→        .map_err(|_| ChannelError::invalid_input(format!("usage: /{command_name} [number]")))
   112→}
   113→
   114→fn is_attachable_session(entry: &SessionEntry) -> bool {
   115→    !entry.archived && !entry.key.starts_with("cron:")
   116→}
   117→
   118→fn session_list_label(entry: &SessionEntry) -> &str {
   119→    entry.label.as_deref().unwrap_or(&entry.key)
   120→}
   121→
   122→fn format_channel_sessions_list(sessions: &[SessionEntry], current_session_key: &str) -> String {
   123→    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   124→    for (i, session) in sessions.iter().enumerate() {
   125→        let marker = if session.key == current_session_key {
   126→            " *"
   127→        } else {
   128→            ""
   129→        };
   130→        lines.push(format!(
   131→            "{}. {} ({} msgs){}",
   132→            i + 1,
   133→            session_list_label(session),
   134→            session.message_count,
   135→            marker,
   136→        ));
   137→    }
   138→    lines.push("\nUse /sessions N to switch.".to_string());
   139→    lines.join("\n")
   140→}
   141→
   142→fn format_attachable_sessions_list(sessions: &[SessionEntry], current_session_key: &str) -> String {
   143→    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   144→    for (i, session) in sessions.iter().enumerate() {
   145→        let label = session_list_label(session);
   146→        let marker = if session.key == current_session_key {
   147→            " *"
   148→        } else {
   149→            ""
   150→        };
   151→        let key_suffix = if label == session.key {
   152→            String::new()
   153→        } else {
   154→            format!(" [{}]", session.key)
   155→        };
   156→        lines.push(format!(
   157→            "{}. {}{} ({} msgs){}",
   158→            i + 1,
   159→            label,
   160→            key_suffix,
   161→            session.message_count,
   162→            marker,
   163→        ));
   164→    }
   165→    lines.push(
   166→        "\nUse /attach N to move an existing session to this chat. This rebinds it from any previous channel chat."
   167→            .to_string(),
   168→    );
   169→    lines.join("\n")
   170→}
   171→
   172→fn format_pending_approvals_list(requests: &[PendingApprovalView]) -> String {
   173→    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   174→    for (i, request) in requests.iter().enumerate() {
   175→        lines.push(format!("{}. `{}`", i + 1, request.command));
   176→    }
   177→    lines.push("\nUse /approve N or /deny N.".to_string());
   178→    lines.join("\n")
   179→}

Now I have a complete understanding of all four fixes. Let me provide my analysis.

---

## Validation and Analysis

### Fix 1: `/attach` -- Scope to same account's sessions

**Your plan is correct and well-reasoned.**

The current code at line 1465 calls `session_metadata.list()`, which returns ALL sessions across all channel types and accounts. The proposed fix replaces it with `session_metadata.list_account_sessions(reply_to.channel_type.as_str(), &reply_to.account_id)`.

**Validation details:**
- `list_account_sessions` exists at `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/sessions/src/metadata.rs:857` and takes `channel_type` and `account_id` parameters, matching by LIKE pattern on `channel_binding` JSON.
- The `is_attachable_session` filter (lines 114-116) only checks `!archived && !key.starts_with("cron:")`, so keeping it is correct -- it filters out system sessions.
- The `reply_to` variable is in scope, with `.channel_type` and `.account_id` available.
- Semantically correct: `/attach` rebinds an existing session to the current chat. Scoping to the same account prevents a user on Bot A's Telegram from seeing/hijacking sessions created via Bot B's WhatsApp.

**Risk: Low.** The change is a single method call substitution. One consideration: `list_account_sessions` matches by `channel_type + account_id` (any chat), which is intentional for `/attach` since you want to pick up sessions from other chats on the same bot.

**No issues found.**

---

### Fix 2: `/approve` and `/deny` -- Require DM context

**Your proposed heuristic (`sender_id != chat_id` implies group) is INCORRECT for 4 of 6 channels.**

My analysis reveals:
- **Telegram:** `sender_id == chat_id` in DMs (chat ID = user ID). Heuristic works.
- **WhatsApp:** `sender_id == chat_id` in DMs (both are the user's JID). Heuristic works.
- **Slack:** `sender_id` is a user ID (`U...`), `chat_id` is a DM channel ID (`D...`). They are **never equal**, even in DMs. Heuristic **fails** -- would block legitimate DM approvals.
- **Discord:** `sender_id` (user ID) != `chat_id` (channel ID), even in DMs. Heuristic **fails**.
- **MS Teams:** `sender_id` (user AAD ID) != `chat_id` (conversation ID), even in DMs. Heuristic **fails**.
- **Matrix:** `sender_id` (`@user:server`) != `chat_id` (`!room:server`), even in DMs. Heuristic **fails**.

**Recommended alternative approach:**

Add an `is_dm: bool` field to `ChannelReplyTarget` with `#[serde(default)]` for backward-compatible deserialization:

```rust
#[serde(default)]
pub is_dm: bool,
```

This requires updating 42 construction sites (across 12 files), but many are in test code and the compiler will flag all of them. Each channel plugin already knows whether it's a DM or group context (they all have `is_group` or equivalent checks). This is the architecturally correct solution.

The `dispatch_command` handler in `channel_events.rs` then checks `reply_to.is_dm` instead of comparing IDs. The `is_dm` field defaults to `false`, meaning existing serialized `channel_binding` entries (which lack the field) will be treated as group context, which is the safe default for security (denying approve/deny in ambiguous contexts).

Wait -- that's actually backwards. If `is_dm` defaults to `false`, then existing DM sessions deserialized from the database would be treated as group context, blocking approve/deny incorrectly. We could instead default to `true` (permissive for existing data), but that weakens the security fix for groups.

A better approach: make it `Option<bool>` with `None` meaning "unknown/legacy" and treat `None` as permissive (allow approve/deny). Only new messages from group contexts would explicitly set `is_dm: Some(false)` and be denied.

Actually, re-reading the requirement more carefully: the concern is about **group chats** where any member could `/approve`. The notification (Fix 3) goes to the session's bound channel. If the session was created in a group, the approval notification goes to that group, and any group member could reply `/approve`.

The simplest effective fix that doesn't require the invasive `ChannelReplyTarget` change: **check if the approval notification's target is a group at the notification level** (only notify in DMs) AND/OR **don't send the full command in group notifications** (which is Fix 3). Combined with Fix 3 truncating the command, the risk is mitigated.

But if we want a proper access-control fix, we need to know the context. Let me propose a **minimal alternative**: modify only `dispatch_command`'s signature in the `ChannelEventSink` trait to add `is_dm: bool`:

```rust
async fn dispatch_command(
    &self,
    command: &str,
    reply_to: ChannelReplyTarget,
    sender_id: Option<&str>,
    is_dm: bool,
) -> Result<String>;
```

This changes 6 implementations + ~15 call sites. The call sites all have `is_group`/`is_dm` already computed. This is much less invasive than modifying `ChannelReplyTarget`.

---

### Fix 3: Truncate command text in approval notifications

**Your plan is correct and straightforward.**

The `command` parameter at `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/gateway/src/approval.rs:135` contains the full shell command string that the LLM generated. This could contain file paths, environment variables, or even secrets (e.g., `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer sk-..."`).

**Important nuance:** The truncation should happen in `notify_origin_channel` (line 135), which sends to the channel. The WebSocket broadcast at line 154-164 (`broadcast_request`) also sends the full command but goes only to authenticated web UI clients, which is acceptable.

**Additional consideration:** The confirmation messages at `channel_events.rs:1596` and `channel_events.rs:1598` (`Approved: \`{}\``, `Denied: \`{}\``) are responses to the person who issued `/approve` or `/deny`. These go back to the same chat where the command was issued. These should also be truncated for consistency, though the risk is lower since the approver already has the full command visible in the `/approvals` list.

**Recommended implementation:**
```rust
fn truncate_command_preview(command: &str, max_len: usize) -> Cow<'_, str> {
    if command.len() <= max_len {
        Cow::Borrowed(command)
    } else {
        Cow::Owned(format!("{}...", &command[..max_len]))
    }
}
```

Use `Cow` per the project's idiom of "use `Cow<'_, str>` when allocation is conditional."

Apply to: `notify_origin_channel` (line 135), and optionally the approve/deny confirmation lines (1596, 1598).

**Risk: Very low.**

---

### Fix 4: MS Teams missing `agent_id` resolution

**Your plan is correct and the pattern is well-established.**

All other channels (WhatsApp at `handlers.rs:383-388`, Discord at `handler.rs:989-992`, Telegram at `handlers.rs:710-713`, Slack at `socket.rs:568-570`, Matrix at `handler.rs:329-331`) use `config.resolve_model(&chat_id, &peer_id)` and `config.resolve_agent_id(&chat_id, &peer_id)`.

MS Teams at `plugin.rs:473-474` uses the raw `config.model.clone()` and hardcodes `agent_id: None`.

**Key observations:**
- `MsTeamsAccountConfig` implements `ChannelConfigView` at `config.rs:259`.
- It implements `channel_model()` (line 284) and `channel_model_provider()` (line 296) with per-team/per-channel override logic.
- It does NOT implement `user_model()`, `user_agent_id()`, `channel_agent_id()`, or `agent_id()` -- these use the trait defaults (return `None`).
- `resolve_model()` calls `user_model > channel_model > model()`. Since MS Teams implements `channel_model()`, using `resolve_model` would actually change behavior if per-channel model overrides exist.

So `config.model.clone()` (current code) only returns the top-level default model. `config.resolve_model(&chat_id, &peer_id)` would also check channel-level overrides. This is a **functional improvement**, not just a cosmetic change.

For `agent_id`: since no override methods are implemented, `resolve_agent_id` will return `None` just like the hardcoded value. But it's future-proof for when team/channel agent overrides are added.

**Correction to your plan:** The method returns `Option<&str>`, so the correct code is:

```rust
model: config.resolve_model(&chat_id, &peer_id).map(String::from),
agent_id: config.resolve_agent_id(&chat_id, &peer_id).map(String::from),
```

Both `chat_id` (String) and `peer_id` (String) are in scope at that point.

**Risk: Low.** For `agent_id`, it's a no-op change. For `model`, it enables per-channel model overrides that were previously silently ignored for MS Teams -- this is a bugfix. A test should verify that `resolve_model` returns per-channel overrides correctly (the test at `config.rs:520` already tests `channel_model()` directly, but a `resolve_model` test for the full chain would be good to add).

---

## Implementation Plan

### Sequencing

These four fixes are independent and can be implemented in any order or in parallel. I recommend:

1. **Fix 1** (trivial one-liner, pure substitution)
2. **Fix 3** (small helper + 1-3 format string changes)
3. **Fix 4** (2 field changes in MsTeams + test)
4. **Fix 2** (most invasive: trait change or `ChannelReplyTarget` field addition)

### Fix 2 Revised Plan

I recommend adding `is_dm: bool` to `dispatch_command`'s trait signature:

1. Add `is_dm: bool` parameter to `ChannelEventSink::dispatch_command` in `crates/channels/src/plugin.rs:351`.
2. Update the 6 implementations (`GatewayChannelEventSink`, `DummySink`, `TestSink` in otp.rs, two `MockSink`s in telegram/discord, and the default impl if any).
3. Update ~15 call sites across channel plugins. Each already has `is_group` or equivalent in scope (negate it for `is_dm`).
4. In `GatewayChannelEventSink::dispatch_command` in `channel_events.rs`, add a guard at the top of the `"approve" | "deny"` arm:
   ```rust
   if !is_dm {
       return Err(ChannelError::invalid_input(
           "/approve and /deny are only available in direct messages. Use the web UI instead."
       ));
   }
   ```

This is cleaner than modifying `ChannelReplyTarget` (42 sites) and keeps the DM/group semantics where they belong (in the command dispatch flow, not in the reply routing struct).

### Tests Needed

- **Fix 1:** Unit test verifying that `/attach` only shows sessions from the same account.
- **Fix 2:** Unit test for the DM guard on approve/deny, and verifying it works when `is_dm = false`.
- **Fix 3:** Unit test for the truncation helper (`truncate_command_preview`).
- **Fix 4:** Test that MS Teams meta uses `resolve_model` (extend existing `config.rs` tests with a `resolve_model` test).

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs` - Fix 1 (`/attach` scoping), Fix 2 (approve/deny DM guard), core command dispatch

For Slack, the command event doesn't expose `channel_type`. Slack DM channel IDs start with `D`, group DMs with `G`, and channels with `C`. This is a reasonable heuristic:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014dLTz82taD6bdY1JqYxdhB
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/slack/src/socket.rs",
  "old_string": "        match sink\n            .dispatch_command(&full_command, reply_to, Some(&sender_id))\n            .await",
  "new_string": "        let is_dm = event.channel_id.to_string().starts_with('D');\n        match sink\n            .dispatch_command(&full_command, reply_to, Some(&sender_id), is_dm)\n            .await"
}

> AGENT

Here's a summary of all the security fixes implemented:

### Fix 1: `/attach` — Scope to same account's sessions
**`crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs`** — Replaced `session_metadata.list()` with `session_metadata.list_account_sessions(channel_type, account_id)`. Now `/attach` only shows sessions bound to the same channel type + account, preventing cross-channel session hijacking.

### Fix 2: `/approve` and `/deny` — Restrict to DM context
**11 files changed** — Added `is_dm: bool` parameter to the `ChannelEventSink::dispatch_command` trait and all implementations/call sites:
- `crates/channels/src/plugin.rs` — trait definition + test mock
- `crates/channels/src/otp.rs` — test mock
- `crates/discord/src/{commands,handler}.rs` — uses `guild_id.is_none()` / `!is_guild`
- `crates/telegram/src/handlers.rs` — uses `matches!(chat_type, ChatType::Dm)` and `ChatKind::Private` for callbacks
- `crates/whatsapp/src/handlers.rs` — uses `!is_group`
- `crates/slack/src/socket.rs` — uses `channel_id.starts_with('D')` (Slack DM channels)
- `crates/matrix/src/handler.rs` — uses `matches!(chat_type, ChatType::Dm)`
- `crates/msteams/src/plugin.rs` — uses `!is_group`
- `crates/gateway/src/channel_events.rs` — added DM guard: `/approve` and `/deny` return an error in group chats directing users to DM or web UI.

### Fix 3: Truncate command in approval notifications
**`crates/gateway/src/approval.rs`** — Added `truncate_command_preview()` helper using `Cow<'_, str>` (per project idioms). Approval channel notifications now truncate commands to 80 chars to prevent leaking secrets. Includes 3 unit tests.

### Fix 4: MS Teams — use `resolve_model` and `resolve_agent_id`
**`crates/gateway/src/msteams/src/plugin.rs`** — Changed from `config.model.clone()` / `agent_id: None` to `config.resolve_model(&chat_id, &peer_id)` / `config.resolve_agent_id(&chat_id, &peer_id)`. This enables per-channel model overrides that were previously silently ignored, and is forward-compatible for agent routing.

> DEVELOPER

What is /approve and /deny exactly?

> AGENT

`/approve` and `/deny` are channel commands (Telegram, WhatsApp, etc.) for managing **exec tool approval requests** — when the LLM agent wants to run a shell command that requires human approval.

### The flow

1. A user chats with the bot in Telegram/WhatsApp, and the agent decides it needs to run a shell command (e.g., `git status`, `curl ...`, `rm -rf /tmp/build`)
2. The command is checked against the approval policy (`crates/tools/src/approval.rs`). If it's not on the allowlist, it enters a **pending approval** state
3. A notification is sent to the originating channel: "Approval needed for `git status`"
4. The user can then:
   - `/approvals` — list pending approval requests for the current session
   - `/approve 2` — approve request #2 from the list
   - `/deny 1` — deny request #1
5. The agent's tool execution either proceeds (approved) or fails (denied)

Previously this was only manageable from the **web UI**. This branch added the channel commands so operators don't have to switch to the browser when a command is waiting for approval — they can resolve it directly from the same Telegram/WhatsApp chat where the conversation is happening.

The security concern I flagged (and fixed) was that in a **group chat**, any group member could `/approve` a dangerous command, not just the person who initiated the conversation.

> DEVELOPER

I see, so now it can only be approved over DMs, can you make it so that any future new channels can *only* have that command sent over "authenticated" channel meaning over DMs or something where the channel knows it's coming from the right person?

> AGENT

Good idea — a `bool` is too easy to get wrong. Let me replace it with a self-documenting enum type so future channel authors see exactly what they're opting into.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/channels/src/plugin.rs",
  "old_string": "use crate::{Error, Result, config_view::ChannelConfigView};\n\n// ── Channel type enum ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────",
  "new_string": "use crate::{Error, Result, config_view::ChannelConfigView};\n\n// ── Sender authentication ───────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Whether the channel can vouch that the sender is authorized for privileged\n/// commands (`/approve`, `/deny`).\n///\n/// Channels **must** set this to [`Verified`](SenderAuth::Verified) only when\n/// the sender's identity is confirmed and they are the sole authorized\n/// participant — typically a direct-message context. Group or channel chats\n/// must always use [`Unverified`](SenderAuth::Unverified) unless the channel\n/// has an independent mechanism to authenticate the sender.\n///\n/// # Adding a new channel\n///\n/// When implementing [`ChannelEventSink::dispatch_command`] for a new channel,\n/// compute the appropriate variant before calling `dispatch_command`:\n///\n/// ```ignore\n/// let auth = SenderAuth::from_dm(!is_group);\n/// sink.dispatch_command(cmd, reply_to, sender_id, auth).await\n/// ```\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SenderAuth {\n    /// The channel has verified the sender is the authorized operator\n    /// (e.g. a direct-message conversation).\n    Verified,\n    /// The sender has **not** been verified for privileged operations\n    /// (e.g. a group chat where any member can send messages).\n    Unverified,\n}\n\nimpl SenderAuth {\n    /// Convenience: convert a \"this is a DM\" boolean into the right variant.\n    pub fn from_dm(is_dm: bool) -> Self {\n        if is_dm {\n            Self::Verified\n        } else {\n            Self::Unverified\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub fn is_verified(self) -> bool {\n        self == Self::Verified\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Channel type enum ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}
```

All passing. Here's what I changed:

Replaced the bare `is_dm: bool` parameter with a `SenderAuth` enum in `crates/channels/src/plugin.rs`:

```rust
pub enum SenderAuth {
    /// The channel has verified the sender is the authorized operator
    /// (e.g. a direct-message conversation).
    Verified,
    /// The sender has **not** been verified for privileged operations
    /// (e.g. a group chat where any member can send messages).
    Unverified,
}
```

Key properties:
- **Self-documenting** — a future channel author sees `SenderAuth::Verified` vs `SenderAuth::Unverified` in the trait signature and the doc comment explains exactly when to use each
- **Convenience helper** — `SenderAuth::from_dm(bool)` makes the common DM-based case easy without hiding the intent
- **Central enforcement** — the gateway rejects `/approve` and `/deny` unless `sender_auth.is_verified()`, so even if a future channel mistakenly passes `Verified` for groups, the pattern makes it obvious during review
- **Extensible** — if a channel later adds its own sender verification (e.g. checking against an operator allowlist), it can set `Verified` without being constrained to "DM only"

Updated across 11 files, all 6 channel implementations + their test mocks.

## Summary: How to Check If a Sender Is on a Channel's Allowlist from Within dispatch_command

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's the complete picture of how to access and check a channel's allowlist from within the gateway's `dispatch_command` handler:

### 1. **ChannelService Trait Definition**
**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/service-traits/src/lib.rs` (lines 192-203)

The `ChannelService` trait has these methods:
```rust
pub trait ChannelService: Send + Sync {
    async fn status(&self) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn logout(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn send(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn add(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn remove(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn update(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn retry_ownership(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn senders_list(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn sender_approve(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
    async fn sender_deny(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult;
}
```

**Note:** The ChannelService doesn't directly expose allowlist checking — this is handled through the `ChannelRegistry` instead.

### 2. **How state.services.channel Is Typed**
**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/gateway/src/services.rs` (line 1408)

```rust
pub struct GatewayServices {
    pub channel: Arc<dyn ChannelService>,
    pub channel_registry: Option<Arc<moltis_channels::ChannelRegistry>>,
    pub channel_store: Option<Arc<dyn moltis_channels::store::ChannelStore>>,
    // ... other fields
}
```

**Key insight:** The `channel_registry` and `channel_store` are what you need for allowlist access, not the `ChannelService` itself.

### 3. **gating::is_allowed Function Signature**
**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/channels/src/gating.rs` (lines 3-21)

```rust
pub fn is_allowed(peer_id: &str, allowlist: &[String]) -> bool {
    if allowlist.is_empty() {
        return true;  // Empty allowlist = everyone is allowed
    }
    let peer_lower = peer_id.to_lowercase();
    allowlist.iter().any(|pattern| {
        let pat = pattern.to_lowercase();
        if pat.contains('*') {
            glob_match(&pat, &peer_lower)  // Supports wildcards like "admin_*"
        } else {
            pat == peer_lower  // Case-insensitive exact match
        }
    })
}
```

**Key behaviors:**
- Empty allowlist = open policy (everyone allowed)
- Case-insensitive matching
- Supports glob wildcards (`admin_*`, `*@example.com`, `user_*_admin`)

### 4. **ChannelConfigView Trait's allowlist() Method**
**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/dorian-pepperberry/crates/channels/src/config_view.rs` (lines 11-87)

```rust
pub trait ChannelConfigView: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug {
    fn allowlist(&self) -> &[String];
    fn group_allowlist(&self) -> &[String];
    fn dm_policy(&self) -> DmPolicy;
    fn group_policy(&self) -> GroupPolicy;
    fn model(&self) -> Option<&str>;
    fn model_provider(&self) -> Option<&str>;
    // ... and resolver methods for user/channel overrides
}
```

### 5. **How to Access Channel Config from dispatch_command**

There are **TWO main patterns** depending on where you are:

#### **Pattern A: Via the ChannelRegistry (Recommended for dispatch_command)**

This is what the existing code uses in `senders_list()` (channel.rs:584-589):

```rust
// Inside dispatch_command or another handler
let state = self.state.get().ok_or_else(|| /* error */)?;

// Get the typed config view
let allowlist = state.services.channel_registry  // Optional
    .as_ref()
    .and_then(|reg| /* need to get account_id from reply_to */)
    .and_then(|reg| {
        // Must await this
        // reg.account_config(account_id).await
        // But can't await in non-async context!
    })
```

**Issue:** The registry's `account_config()` is async, so you need an async context.

#### **Pattern B: Via the ChannelStore (Direct JSON access)**

This is what the existing code uses in `sender_approve()` (channel.rs:650-661):

```rust
let stored = state.services.channel_store  // Optional
    .as_ref()
    .ok_or(/* error */)?
    .get(channel_type.as_str(), account_id)
    .await
    .map_err(/* error */)?
    .ok_or(/* error */)?;

let config = stored.config;  // Raw serde_json::Value

// Access allowlist directly from JSON
let allowlist = config
    .as_object()
    .and_then(|obj| obj.get("allowlist"))
    .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
    .map(|arr| arr.iter().filter_map(|v| v.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>())
    .unwrap_or_default();

// Use gating::is_allowed from moltis_channels::gating
use moltis_channels::gating::is_allowed;
if is_allowed(sender_id, &allowlist) {
    // Allow this sender
}
```

### 6. **Full Pattern from dispatch_command Context**

Looking at `dispatch_command` in channel_events.rs (lines 1157-1162):

```rust
async fn dispatch_command(
    &self,
    command: &str,
    reply_to: ChannelReplyTarget,
    sender_id: Option<&str>,
    sender_auth: SenderAuth,
) -> ChannelResult<String> {
    let state = self.state.get()
        .ok_or_else(|| ChannelError::unavailable("gateway not ready"))?;
```

**Complete example of checking allowlist in dispatch_command:**

```rust
// Get the account_id from reply_to
let account_id = &reply_to.account_id;
let sender_id = sender_id.unwrap_or(&reply_to.chat_id);

// Option 1: Using registry (if you need typed config)
if let Some(registry) = &state.services.channel_registry {
    if let Some(config_view) = registry.account_config(account_id).await {
        let allowlist = config_view.allowlist();
        use moltis_channels::gating::is_allowed;
        if !is_allowed(sender_id, allowlist) {
            return Err(ChannelError::forbidden("sender not on allowlist"));
        }
    }
}

// Option 2: Using store (raw JSON, simpler for dispatch_command)
if let Some(store) = &state.services.channel_store {
    // Resolve channel_type - this depends on your dispatch context
    if let Ok(Some(stored)) = store.get(channel_type_str, account_id).await {
        let allowlist_key = if channel_type == ChannelType::Matrix {
            "user_allowlist"
        } else {
            "allowlist"
        };
        
        if let Some(allowlist_array) = stored.config
            .as_object()
            .and_then(|obj| obj.get(allowlist_key))
            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
        {
            let allowlist: Vec<String> = allowlist_array
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(String::from))
                .collect();
            
            use moltis_channels::gating::is_allowed;
            if !is_allowed(sender_id, &allowlist) {
                return Err(ChannelError::forbidden("sender not on allowlist"));
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### Key Takeaways

1. **Registry path** (`registry.account_config()`) returns a typed `ChannelConfigView` — cleaner but requires async
2. **Store path** (`store.get()`) returns raw JSON — more direct but requires manual field parsing
3. **Allowlist is optional** — call `.unwrap_or_default()` or check `.is_none()` to handle missing config
4. **Empty allowlist = open** — the `is_allowed()` function treats empty lists as allowing everyone
5. **Matrix is special** — uses `user_allowlist` instead of `allowlist` as the config key
6. **Use `moltis_channels::gating::is_allowed()`** — don't implement your own matching logic; it handles glob patterns

    23→/// Simple glob matching supporting `*` as a wildcard for any sequence of chars.
    24→fn glob_match(pattern: &str, text: &str) -> bool {
    25→    let parts: Vec<&str> = pattern.split('*').collect();
    26→    if parts.len() == 1 {
    27→        return pattern == text;
    28→    }
    29→

    31→    for (i, part) in parts.iter().enumerate() {
    32→        if part.is_empty() {
    33→            continue;
    34→        }
    35→        match text[pos..].find(part) {
    36→            Some(idx) => {
    37→                // First segment must match at start
    38→                if i == 0 && idx != 0 {
    39→                    return false;
    40→                }

Now I'll remove `SenderAuth` entirely from the trait and move the authorization check into the gateway. The approach:

1. Remove `SenderAuth` enum and `sender_auth` param from the trait
2. In the gateway's approve/deny handler, look up the channel config via `channel_registry.account_config()` and check `sender_id` against the allowlist
3. Non-empty allowlist required — if the channel is open (no allowlist), only the web UI can approve

Group or channel chats\n/// must always use [`Unverified`](SenderAuth::Unverified) unless the channel\n/// has an independent mechanism to authenticate the sender.\n///\n/// # Adding a new channel\n///\n/// When implementing [`ChannelEventSink::dispatch_command`] for a new channel,\n/// compute the appropriate variant before calling `dispatch_command`:\n///\n/// ```ignore\n/// let auth = SenderAuth::from_dm(!is_group);\n/// sink.dispatch_command(cmd, reply_to, sender_id, auth).await\n/// ```\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SenderAuth {\n    /// The channel has verified the sender is the authorized operator\n    /// (e.g. a direct-message conversation).\n    Verified,\n    /// The sender has **not** been verified for privileged operations\n    /// (e.g. a group chat where any member can send messages).\n    Unverified,\n}\n\nimpl SenderAuth {\n    /// Convenience: convert a \"this is a DM\" boolean into the right variant.\n    pub fn from_dm(is_dm: bool) -> Self {\n        if is_dm {\n            Self::Verified\n        } else {\n            Self::Unverified\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub fn is_verified(self) -> bool {\n        self == Self::Verified\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Channel type enum ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────",

> AGENT

Good. Now let me think about the user's message carefully. They want:

1. **Admin role** — anyone on the DM allowlist is an "admin" who can run privileged commands from any context (DM or group)
2. **Channel command allowlist** — a config option to specify which commands non-admins can run from channels (e.g., `/context`, `/compact`)
3. **Denial feedback** — when a command is denied, tell the user via a reply

This is a bigger design change. Let me address the user directly about scope before implementing.

---

Great points. Let me make sure I understand the model you're describing:

1. **DM allowlist = admin**: anyone on a channel's `allowlist` is treated as an admin — they can run *all* commands from anywhere (DM, group, etc.)
2. **Channel commands config**: a separate config (e.g., `channel_commands = ["context", "compact", "sessions", ...]`) that controls which commands non-admin users can run from group chats
3. **Denial feedback**: when a non-admin tries a restricted command, reply in the same chat (reply/thread) saying they're not authorized

Before I build all of that, I want to flag a scoping question: the channel command allowlist config is a meaningful feature addition — new config field in each channel's config, schema updates, validation, template updates, docs. That feels like it should be a separate issue/PR rather than part of this security fix branch.

What I'd suggest for **this PR**:

- Implement the admin check now: for privileged commands (`/approve`, `/deny`), verify `sender_id` is on the channel's `allowlist` via `channel_registry.account_config()`. If not, reply with a denial message in the same chat.
- For the channel command allowlist config — create a follow-up issue since it touches config schema, validation, template, and docs.

Does that split make sense, or do you want both in this PR?

> DEVELOPER

no, what I meant is anyone allowing to DM moltis should be allowed to execute commands also on the channels. But other members in channels where Moltis is should not be allowed to DMs or execute commands on the channels. Forget admin roles it's too complex, moltis is supposed to be a personal assistant not a generic bot, meaning it will probably only have 1 allowed members for DMs even if that owner invites his bot to a channel so others can talk to his bot.

> AGENT

Clear — the DM allowlist is the source of truth. If you're on it, you can run any command from anywhere. If not, you can chat but can't run privileged commands. Let me implement that.

> AGENT

Here's the final state of all four security fixes:

### Fix 1: `/attach` — scoped to same account
`list()` → `list_account_sessions(channel_type, account_id)` — prevents cross-channel session hijacking.

### Fix 2: `/approve` and `/deny` — allowlist-based authorization
The `is_sender_on_allowlist()` helper checks `sender_id` against the channel account's DM `allowlist` via `channel_registry.account_config()`. The model:
- **On the allowlist** → can `/approve` and `/deny` from anywhere (DMs or groups)
- **Not on the allowlist** → can chat (if group policy allows) but cannot run privileged commands
- **Empty allowlist** (open DM policy) → no one is explicitly authorized, only web UI can approve

No changes to the `dispatch_command` trait — channels don't need to think about this. Authorization is enforced centrally in the gateway.

### Fix 3: Truncate command in approval notifications
`truncate_command_preview()` caps channel notifications at 80 chars to prevent leaking secrets.

### Fix 4: MS Teams — use `resolve_model` and `resolve_agent_id`
Consistent with all other channels.
